| You are given an HTML string. Your task is to determine the number of characters encompassed by each HTML tag in the string. For each tag, count the number of characters that are enclosed between the opening (<tag>) and closing (</tag>) tags.<br><br>A tag is composed of one or more lowercase English letters (a-z). For example: `<div> ... </div>`, `<span>..</span>`. It can be followed by digits (0-9) i.e `<h1>..</h1>`.<br><br>The content between the tags can consist of:<br>1. English characters (a-z, A-Z).<br>2. Digits (0-9).<br>3. Punctuation marks: period (.), comma (,), and spaces(' ').<br><br>Note:<br>When a character is encompassed by a same tag multiple times, count only once for that tag.<br>A tag can have 0 characters. In that case don't print that tag.<br>Spaces(' ') between the tags are not counted.<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://www.hackerrank.com/contests/orbitax-associate-software-engineer-recruitment-2024-phase-1/challenges/count-between-tags/problem) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>string consume(string &s,int st){<br>    string tag;<br>    while(s[st]!='>') tag+=s[st++];<br>    return tag;<br>}<br><br>void solve(string s) {<br>    // the total character inside a tag<br>    map<string,int> totalCharCount;<br>    // to find the nesting level of a tag<br>    // eg. <p><p></p></p>, here p is nested two times<br>    map<string,int> nestedTagLevel;<br><br>    vector<string> tags;<br>    vector<int> charCount;<br><br>    // signifies root level tag<br>    // helps to simplify code logic<br>    tags.push_back("");<br>    charCount.push_back(0);<br><br>    for(int i=0;i<s.size();i++){<br>        if( s[i] == '<' and s[i+1]!='/'  ){<br>            // starting tag<br>            string tag = consume(s,i+1);<br>            // advance pointer by the consumer<br>            // character count<br>            i+=tag.size()+1;<br>            tags.push_back(tag);<br>            charCount.push_back(0);<br>            nestedTagLevel[tag]++;<br>        }else if( s[i] == '<' and s[i+1] == '/' ){<br>            // ending tag<br>            string tag = consume(s,i+2);<br>             // advance pointer by the consumer<br>            // character count<br>            i+=tag.size()+2;<br>            int cnt = charCount.back();<br>            nestedTagLevel[tag]--;<br><br>            // increment count only if it has no parent<br>            // tag of same type<br>            if( nestedTagLevel[tag] == 0 ){<br>                totalCharCount[tags.back()] += cnt;<br>            }<br>            charCount.pop_back();<br>            tags.pop_back();<br>            // propagate the character count to its<br>            // parents too<br>            charCount.back()+=cnt;<br><br>        }else{<br>            if(s[i] != ' ') charCount.back()++;<br>        }<br>    }<br><br>    for(auto [tag,cnt]:totalCharCount){<br>        if(cnt) {<br>            cout<<tag<<": "<<cnt<<endl;<br>        }<br>    }<br>}<br>``` |

| You are given a string S of length N and an integer K. Your task is to find the total number of subsequences in S that match the pattern P = "orbitaxian", where the difference in position between every consecutive character in the subsequence is no more than K.<br><br>More formally, let the subsequence of P be represented by an array pos, where (1 ≤ pos[i] ≤ N) and S[pos[i]] = P[i]. Then for each i > 1, the condition pos[i] − pos[i-1] ≤ K must hold.<br><br>The result should be returned modulo 10^9+7<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://www.hackerrank.com/contests/orbitax-associate-software-engineer-recruitment-2024-phase-1/challenges/orbitax-sub-sequence) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>#define M 1000000007<br>map<char,queue<pii> > pos;<br>map<char,int> cntStack;<br>int k;<br>void calc(char prev, char cur,int ind){<br>    while(pos[prev].size() and ind-pos[prev].front().first>k) {<br>        cntStack[prev]-=pos[prev].front().second;<br>        cntStack[prev] %= M;<br>        cntStack[prev] += M;<br>        cntStack[prev] %= M;<br><br>        pos[prev].pop();<br>    }<br>    if( pos[prev].size() ) {<br>        pos[cur].push({ind,cntStack[prev]});<br>        cntStack[cur]+=cntStack[prev];<br>        cntStack[cur] %= M;<br>    }<br>}<br><br>void solve() {<br>    string orbitax = "orbitaxIAn";<br>    pos.clear();<br>    cntStack.clear();<br>    int n;<br>    cin>>n>>k;<br>    string s;<br>    cin>>s;<br>    for(int i=0;i<s.size();i++){<br>        char cur = s[i];<br>        char prev;<br>        if( s[i] == 'o' ){<br>            pos[cur].push({i,1});<br>            cntStack[cur]++;<br>        }else if( s[i] == 'r' ){<br>            calc('o','r',i);<br>        }else if( s[i] == 'b' ){<br>            calc('r','b',i);<br>        }else if( s[i] == 'i' ){<br>            calc('b','i',i);<br>            calc('x','I',i);<br>        }else if( s[i] == 't' ){<br>            calc('i','t',i);<br>        }else if( s[i] == 'a' ){<br>            calc('t','a',i);<br>            calc('I','A',i);<br>        }else if( s[i] == 'x' ){<br>            calc('a','x',i);<br>        }else if( s[i] == 'n' ){<br>            calc('A','n',i);<br>        }<br>    }<br>    cout<<cntStack['n']<<endl;<br>}<br><br>signed main() {<br>    FASTIO;<br>    int tc=1;<br>    cin>>tc;<br>    while(tc--) solve();<br>}<br>``` |

| You are given an array of weights of length 200 and 3 buckets. The sum of total weights of the array will not exceed 100. You need to distribute the weights among the buckets such that the maximum value of the difference between the sum of weights in any two bucket is minimum. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>bool dp[205][505][505];<br>// until index i, weights on 1st bucket is j and 2nd bucket is k,<br>// and 3rd bucket is cumsum[i]-j-k;<br>void solve() {<br>    int n;<br>    cin>>n;<br>    int weights[n+1];<br>    int csum = 0;<br>    for(int i=1;i<=n;i++)<br>        cin>>weights[i];<br><br>    // we can put 0 weight in 1st,2nd and 3rd bucket with 0 weights<br>    dp[0][0][0] = true;<br><br>    for(int i=1;i<=n;i++){<br>        for(int j=0;j<=min(csum,500);j++){<br>            for(int k=0;k<=min(csum,500);k++){<br>                if( !dp[i-1][j][k] ) continue;<br>                // put it in 1st bucket<br>                dp[i][j][k] = true;<br>                // put it in the second bucket<br>                if( j+weights[i]<=500 ) dp[i][j+weights[i]][k] = true;<br>                // put it in the third bucket<br>                if( k+weights[i]<=500 ) dp[i][j][k+weights[i]] = true;<br>            }<br>        }<br>        csum+=weights[i];<br>    }<br>    cout<<csum<<endl;<br>    int mx = -1;<br>    for(int i=0;i<=min(csum,500);i++){<br>        for(int j=0;j<=min(csum,500);j++){<br>            if( dp[n][i][j] == false ) continue;<br>            int k = csum-i-j;<br>            cout<<i<<" "<<j<<" "<<k<<endl;<br>            int val = max( {abs(i-j),abs(j-k),abs(k-i)} );<br>            mx = mx == -1? val: min(mx,val);<br>        }<br>    }<br>    cout<<mx<<endl;<br>}<br>``` |

# Optimizely Bangladesh

|  |  |
| :-| :- |
| Founding year | |
| Company Website | https://www.optimizely.com/ |
| Career Website | https://careers.optimizely.com/search/ |
| Technologies Used| Python, NodeJS, Angular2, Scala, MongoDB |

## Introduction

[Optimizely](https://www.optimizely.com/get-started/) has recently expanded its global operations with the establishment of a new office in Bangladesh. Optimizely is a leading software company specializing in digital experience platforms (DXP) that empower businesses to enhance their marketing and product strategies. Optimizely offers a comprehensive suite of tools, including a robust Content Management System (CMS) and an integrated Content Marketing Platform (CMP).

> [!NOTE]
> Optimizely was part of the Y Combinator (YC) Winter 2010 batch. This is one of largest global companies which have office in Bangladesh.

## Interview Stages

#### Software Engineer Intern

For the Software Engineer Intern position, the interview process consists of the following stages:

1. **Phone Screening**: The first stage of the interview process is a phone screening with a recruiter. The recruiter will ask you about your background, experience, and interest in the company. This is also an opportunity for you to ask questions about the role and the company.

2. **Take-Home Assignment**: If you pass the phone screening, you will be given a take-home assignment to complete. It's more of a practical problem-solving task that evaluates your coding skills and problem-solving abilities. You will need to come up with the solution, write a clean code, testcases(edge cases are important), and submit it in a google form. It will be judged based on correctness, efficiency, and code quality.

3. **On-Site Interview**: If you successfully complete the take-home assignment, you will be invited for an on-site interview. This will be a system design interview where you will be asked to design and code a system in 1 hour. Then you will be asked about your code, database design, sql queries, basic networking and OS, OOP concepts, design patterns and project related knowledge. There may be some in depth questions about the technologies in the projects that you have worked on. The whole interview may take around 2.5 hours. If you pass this stage, there may be a final interview with the hiring manager or a behavioral interview.

## Take-Home Assignment Questions

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Build a tshirt distribution system | Imagine you're working at a T-shirt factory and you manage orders by packing a bulk amount of shirts into a certain number of bags. For every order, you have a specification of how many bags you have to fill and a fixed number of shirts to fill them with. The requirement is to distribute the shirts in the bags as evenly as possible.<br><br>For example, if there are 100 shirts and 10 bags, then each bag will get 10 shirts. However, sometimes the shirt factory will have production errors, and it might produce the wrong number of shirts. It's not going to be 200 shirts instead of 100, but there will be some minor deviation from the original number.<br><br>Write a function to solve this problem. Define the function name, input parameters, and output type as you wish. The goal is to minimize the deviation of the number of shirts in each bag, i.e., distribute as evenly as possible, with the important aspect that only a single bag can have a different number of shirts. For example, if you have 10 bags and 103 shirts, then 9 bags can have 10 shirts, and the last bag can have 13 shirts. Here the deviation is 13 - 10 = 3, which is the minimum deviation. |  |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Task Management Software Reviewer Assignment | Let's say you are building a task management software (get familiar with JIRA, Trello, or Asana). Each task in the system has a few properties:<br>- `taskId`: An integer identifier.<br>- `assigneeName`: The person who is assigned to or responsible for the task.<br>- `reviewerName`: The person responsible for reviewing the task (think code review) - this can be null.<br>- `status`: The current state of the task, which could be "todo", "in-progress", "in-review", or "done".<br>- `estimateInHours`: The number of hours estimated to complete this task for the assignee.<br><br>Assume that you are given the list of tasks for a single team. We want to build a function that would do a simple recommendation for reviewers for each task. Given the list of tasks with the basic properties, our function should return a modified list where each task has a non-null `reviewerName` property, given that the task does not already have an assigned reviewer. The function should ensure that the reviewers are distributed as evenly as possible across all tasks and that the total effort for each team member is minimized.<br><br>Constraints / Assumptions:<br><br>- Some tasks in the list will already have a reviewer assigned to them.<br>- You can assume all the tasks have one person assigned.<br>- You can assume that everyone on the team has at least one task assigned to them.<br>- You should only suggest reviewers for tasks that are in the "in-review" status.<br>- You should try to distribute reviewers as evenly as possible across all the tasks, <br>  so that one person does not review too many tasks.<br>- You should try to minimize the total effort in terms of the number of hours<br>  for each team member. <br>Assume that reviewing a task takes 1/3 of the `estimateInHours` for that task. |  |

## On-Site Interview Questions

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design a Backend for a Simplified Version of Internet Banking | #### Acceptance Criteria:<br><br>- **View Account Balance**: Users can view their current account balance.<br>- **Transfer Money**: Users can transfer money to another registered user by providing the recipient's phone number and amount.<br>- **Pay Utility Bill**: Users can pay their utility bill to utility providers. They can choose the provider from a list of providers in the system.<br>- **Top N Users by Transactions**: Given a list of transactions, write a function that would return the top N users based on the number of transactions, in descending order of transaction volume. The result should include the user name, phone number, and number of transactions.<br>- **View Transaction History**: Users can view their transaction history, ordered by most recent. The history should include all kinds of transactions. For user transactions, it should include the recipient’s phone number and the amount. For bill payments, it should include the utility provider name. All transactions should also have the payment date. | **Bonus Problem**:<br>- **Promo Codes Management**: Admin users can create promo codes in the system, which users can use while paying bills. Each promo code can be used a certain number of times per user, and each promo code has a certain amount of cashback.<br>- **Promo Codes Analytics**: Admin users can see a list of all promo codes, including the total number of uses, the average number of times utilized per user, and the total amount of cashback disbursed for each promo code. This list should be ordered by the most recent promo code first. |

## Benefits, Perks and Things to Consider
Optimizely is a certified Great Place to Work. Here are some of the benefits and perks they offer:
- **Flexible Work Arrangements:** Optimizely supports flexible work from home or onsite work arrangements.
- **Parental leave:** Optimizely offers a supportive parental leave for both birthing and non-birthing parents. Duration of leave may vary by local regulations.
- **Meals and Snacks:** Free snacks and drinks are available in the office.
- **Transportation:** Optimizely provides transportation facilities for employees commuting to the office.
- **Health and Wellness:** Optimizely offers health insurance and wellness programs to support the well-being of its employees.
- **Provident Fund:** Optimizely contributes to the provident fund for its employees, ensuring financial security for the future.
- **Bonuses:** Optimizely offers 2 festival bonuses each year.



<!-- START OF SECTION: companies/pathao.md -->

# Pathao

|                   |                                     |
| :---------------- | :---------------------------------- |
| Founding year     | 2015                                |
| Company Website   | https://pathao.com                  |
| Career Website    | https://career.pathao.com/#position |
| Technologies Used |                                     |

## Introduction
Pathao, started as an app based user friendly courier service provider, now a super-app including ride sharing,rentals,food delivery,groceries and so on. They have also their own digital wallet named pathay pay and upcoming products like pathao maps.

## Interview Stages:

These are the steps for the recent Associate Software Engineer (Front end) hiring
1. **Initial Interview**:	In-person 
2. **Take Home Assessment**:   A task to perform and submit in github
3. **On-site Assessment**:	A task to perform and show on-site
4. **Final Interview**: Final round with CTO and HR

## Initial Interview:

In the initial interview they asked the applicants from their resume, their projects and experience mostly. They are speicifically looking for expertise in `JavaScript` and `React` so they asked some basic concepts like difference between `context` and `state`, difference between `zustand` and `redux`, what is `reducer` function, how `useEffect` hook is used and how does it work.

## Take Home Assessment:

A week after the initial interview, pathao sends an email with proper instruction to build a trello like dashboard with boards,columns and drag-and-drop support. A task should be allowed to add,move edit and delete within the boards. There were bonus points for reordering in the same column, saving across reloads, using OAuth login, unit tests etc. 
- [This](https://github.com/RadifTajwar/Pathao) is the repository of the take home assessment.

## On-site Assessment:

On completing the take home assessment successfully, they asked for an on-site assessment of 5-6 hours in the next week. This time they asked to create an e-commerce dashboard system with basic `CRUD` operations. They asked it to create with `nuxt` js as their existing system is made with `vuejs`. 
- [This](https://github.com/RadifTajwar/pathaoFinal) is the repository of the final assessment.

## Final Interview:

In this round they asked about how the on-site assessment was done. How difficult it was to use a different framework nuxt, as a react/nextjs developer. Asked some questions about graph data structures and algorithms. But most of the qustions of this round were behavioral and non-technical.

## Contributor
- [Radif Tajwar](https://bd.linkedin.com/in/radiftajwar)

provided as a function parameter (Blue block in the example).<br>- Blocks with a value of -1 are impassable (Grey blocks in the example).<br>- The Goal block and only the Goal block will always have a cost of 0 (Green blocks in the example).<br>- The maze will not have any circular routes that do not go through the Goal block first.<br>- The maze is stored in a 2D array. The 2D maze array will be defined globally.<br>- The maze size will be a pair of positive integer numbers, you can assume that the size parameters, length, and height of the maze will fit in a C integer (int) type.<br>- The cost values, excluding the impassable blocks, will always be a positive integer that will fit in a C integer (int) type.<br>- The maze length and height are not guaranteed to be equal but will be global fixed defined constants for each problem. |  |  |

## Introduction
[RoBenDevs](https://robendevs.com//) specializes in building SaaS solutions. 
## Interview Stages
RoBenDevs has a 3 stage interview process for Software Engineer, Intern role.
1. **Initial Screening:** A project description is given to the candidate. The candidate is expected to complete the project within given timeframe. The project is then reviewed by the team.
2. **Problem Solving:** The candidate is given several problems to solve. The problems are mostly related to data structures and algorithms. The candidate is expected to explain the solution and the reasoning behind it.
3. **Whiteboard System Design:** The candidate is tasked with designing a system on a whiteboard from a high level perspective. The candidate is expected to explain the reasoning behind the design.

[Rokomari](https://www.rokomari.com) is Bangladesh’s leading online bookstore platform, offering millions of books from local and international publishers along with gadgets, stationery, and more. With fast delivery, secure payment, and exclusive deals, Rokomari has become the go-to destination for book readers.

In this article, the questions of recruitment test for Backend Developer role at Rokomari is presented. The test was taken on 13th September, 2025 at their Motijheel Office.

## Interview Stages
- **Written test**: The total time is 2 hours.
- **On site Interview**

## Written Test Questions

- Basic scenario based questions from backend technology
- Complex SQL query (15 marks)

### Problem Solving (60 Marks)


| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| You are given a tree with n nodes and e edges. Each node has a value. Then you are given Q queries. In each query, you are given a node number, and you need to return the XOR of all nodes in the subtree rooted at that node (including the node itself). |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>#include <bits/stdc++.h><br>using namespace std;<br><br>const int MAXN = 100005;<br>vector<int> adj[MAXN];<br>int subXor[MAXN];<br><br>void dfs(int u, int p) {<br>    subXor[u] = u;<br>    for (int v : adj[u]) {<br>        if (v == p) continue;<br>        dfs(v, u);<br>        subXor[u] ^= subXor[v];<br>    }<br>}<br><br>int main() {<br>    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);<br>    cin.tie(nullptr);<br><br>    int n, e;<br>    cin >> n >> e;<br><br>    while(e--) {<br>        int u, v; cin >> u >> v;<br>        adj[u].push_back(v);<br>        adj[v].push_back(u);<br>    }<br><br>    dfs(1, -1);<br><br>    int Q; cin >> Q;<br>    while (Q--) {<br>        int node; cin >> node;<br>        cout << subXor[node] << "\n";<br>    }<br>}<br>``` |


| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A very large number (length between 50 to 100 digits) in string format is given. You need to check if the number is divisible by 7. Solve this for t test cases. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>#include <bits/stdc++.h><br>using namespace std;<br><br>bool divisibleBy7(const string &num) {<br>    int rem = 0;<br>    for (char c : num) {<br>        rem = (rem * 10 + (c - '0')) % 7;<br>    }<br>    return rem == 0;<br>}<br><br>int main() {<br>    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);<br>    cin.tie(nullptr);<br><br>    int t; cin >> t;<br>    while (t--) {<br>        string num; cin >> num;<br>        cout << (divisibleBy7(num) ? "YES" : "NO") << "\n";<br>    }<br>}<br>``` |


| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Implement Merge Sort for t test cases. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>#include <bits/stdc++.h><br>using namespace std;<br><br>void merge(vector<int> &a, int l, int m, int r) {<br>    vector<int> left(a.begin() + l, a.begin() + m + 1);<br>    vector<int> right(a.begin() + m + 1, a.begin() + r + 1);<br><br>    int i = 0, j = 0, k = l;<br>    while (i < left.size() && j < right.size()) {<br>        if (left[i] <= right[j]) a[k++] = left[i++];<br>        else a[k++] = right[j++];<br>    }<br>    while (i < left.size()) a[k++] = left[i++];<br>    while (j < right.size()) a[k++] = right[j++];<br>}<br><br>void mergeSort(vector<int> &a, int l, int r) {<br>    if (l >= r) return;<br>    int m = (l + r) / 2;<br>    mergeSort(a, l, m);<br>    mergeSort(a, m + 1, r);<br>    merge(a, l, m, r);<br>}<br><br>int main() {<br>    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);<br>    cin.tie(nullptr);<br><br>    int t; cin >> t;<br>    while (t--) {<br>        int n; cin >> n;<br>        vector<int> arr(n);<br>        for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) cin >> arr[i];<br>        mergeSort(arr, 0, n - 1);<br>        for (int x : arr) cout << x << " ";<br>        cout << "\n";<br>    }<br>}<br>``` |


| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A non-decreasing array is given. A new group is formed whenever the difference between two consecutive numbers is greater than 1 (the later number starts a new group). You are allowed to remove any elements from the array to maximize the number of groups. Return the maximum number of groups you can form. Solve this for t test cases.<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://codeforces.com/problemset/problem/2114/C) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>#include <bits/stdc++.h><br>using namespace std;<br><br>int maxGroups(vector<int>& arr) {<br>    int n = arr.size();<br>    int groups = 0;<br>    for (int i = 0; i < n;) {<br>        groups++;<br>        int last = arr[i];<br>        while (i < n && arr[i] - last <= 1) i++;<br>    }<br>    return groups;<br>}<br><br>int main() {<br>    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);<br>    cin.tie(nullptr);<br><br>    int t; cin >> t;<br>    while (t--) {<br>        int n; cin >> n;<br>        vector<int> arr(n);<br>        for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) cin >> arr[i];<br>        sort(arr.begin(), arr.end());<br>        cout << maxGroups(arr) << "\n";<br>    }<br>}<br>``` |


| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| For a given number, find and print all of its distinct prime divisors. Solve this for t test cases. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>#include <bits/stdc++.h><br>using namespace std;<br><br>vector<long long> primeDivisors(long long n) {<br>    vector<long long> primes;<br>    for (long long i = 2; i * i <= n; i++) {<br>        if (n % i == 0) {<br>            primes.push_back(i);<br>            while (n % i == 0) n /= i;<br>        }<br>    }<br>    if (n > 1) primes.push_back(n);<br>    return primes;<br>}<br><br>int main() {<br>    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);<br>    cin.tie(nullptr);<br><br>    int t; cin >> t;<br>    while (t--) {<br>        long long n; cin >> n;<br>        vector<long long> primes = primeDivisors(n);<br>        for (long long p : primes) cout << p << " ";<br>        cout << "\n";<br>    }<br>}<br>``` |

| --- | --- | --- |

Explain each stage and how they work together to create a runnable program. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>![](https://d8it4huxumps7.cloudfront.net/uploads/images/655df16819a37_compilation_in_c_01.jpg?d=2000x2000)<br>- **Preprocessing:** Handles directives like `#include` and `#define`.  <br>- **Compilation:** Converts source code into assembly code.  <br>- **Assembling:** Translates assembly code into machine code (object file).  <br>- **Linking:** Combines object files and libraries to produce the final executable. |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Define the following fundamental computer science terms: DMA, Byte Order, coroutine, RISC, PCB. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>- **DMA (Direct Memory Access):** A feature that allows hardware devices to access main memory without CPU intervention.  <br>- **Byte Order (Endianness):** The order in which bytes are stored for multi-byte data types; primarily little-endian vs. big-endian.  <br>- **Coroutine:** A generalization of subroutines allowing execution to be paused and resumed; useful for cooperative multitasking.  <br>- **RISC (Reduced Instruction Set Computer):** A CPU design philosophy focusing on a small, highly optimized set of instructions.  <br>- **PCB (Process Control Block):** A data structure used by the operating system to store all information about a process (e.g., PID, state, registers, memory info). |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| List and briefly describe the major parts of a CPU (Central Processing Unit): |  | **Show Answer**:<br>- **ALU (Arithmetic Logic Unit):** Performs arithmetic and logical operations.  <br>- **CU (Control Unit):** Directs the operation of the processor by interpreting instructions.  <br>- **Registers:** Small, fast memory locations for temporary data storage during instruction execution.  <br>- **Cache:** A small-sized, high-speed memory for frequently accessed data.  <br>- **Clock:** Synchronizes all operations and controls the pace of instruction execution. |

## Introduction
ShopUp is a B2B e-commerce platform connecting small retailers to manufacturers and suppliers, digitizing supply chain. 

## Interview Stages:

These are the steps for the ShopUp Fast track launchpad
1. **Aptitude Test**:	Online aptitude test
2. **SQL Test**:   Online SQL test

## Aptitude Test:

The first round was an
online aptitude test consisting of 23 questions to be solved within 1 hour. The questions covered
topics like pattern matching, profit and interest calculations, and age-related math problems
(e.g., father and children age problems).

## SQL Test:

There were three SQL problems to solve and record a video
explaining the thought process, and submit via a Google Form. Two of the problems were
very challenging—comparable to the "Hard" category on HackerRank.

## In Person Interviews:

# REVE Systems

|  | |
| :-| :- |
| Founding year | 2003 |
| Company Website | https://www.revesoft.com/ |
| Career Website | https://www.revesoft.com/ |
| Technologies Used| Java, Kotlin, Swift |

## Introduction
[REVE Systems](https://www.revesoft.com/) specializes in delivering VoIP (Voice over IP) software solutions, with a focus on mobile VoIP, softswitch, and billing solutions. They have different departments such as Research & Development (R&D), E-Gov, Reve Chat, etc. 

In this article, the recruitment process for the R&D department for the DEV roles (Junior Software Engineer) is presented. They perform campus recruitment.

## Interview Stages
1. **Online Screening Test**: This round is conducted via Zoom. All the participants are required to keep their microphones and cameras on during the MCQ exam via Google form. Questions were given from the following areas:

    1. Basic problem solving
    1. Data structure and algorithms
    1. Time Complexity
    1. Finding error in code snippets
    1. Finding the output of code blocks
    1. Object-oriented programming
    1. SQL
    1. Computer networking
    1. Software engineering and design patterns

1. **Technical Round I**: Two interviewers conducted this round via Skype. During the whole interview, the camera and microphone have to be on. At the beginning of the interview, a Google Doc might be shared for writing the code or any answers. Candidates are not allowed to use a pen or paper. Only the shared doc has to be used.

1. **Technical Round II**: This round is also conducted via Skype, and two interviewers (project managers) might be there. This is a bit more technical than round 1. Topics may include - Java socket programming, computer networking, focusing on TCP and UDP protocols, software engineering and design patterns, SOLID principles, string matching algorithms      (Naive, KMP).
4. **CTO Round**: This will be an onsite round where the CTO and any other senior engineer. Candidates might be asked to solve problems using pen and paper.

## Technical Round I Questions
| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Reverse a given singly linked list.<br>  <br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/reverse-linked-list/description/)<br><br>At first, I used extra memory to store the reversed array. |  | **Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>class Solution {<br>public:<br>    ListNode* reverseList(ListNode* head) {<br>        if (head == NULL \|\| head-&gt;next == NULL)<br>            return head;<br>        <br>        int i;<br>        vector&lt;int&gt; v;<br>        ListNode* cur = head;<br>        while (cur != NULL) {<br>            v.push_back(cur-&gt;val);<br>            cur = cur-&gt;next;<br>        }<br>        <br>        i = v.size()-1;<br>        cur = head;<br>        while (head != NULL) {<br>            head-&gt;val = v[i];<br>            i--;<br>            head = head-&gt;next;<br>        }<br>        return cur;<br>    }<br>};</code></pre><br><br>**Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>class Solution {<br>public:<br>    ListNode* reverseList(ListNode* head) {<br>        if (head == NULL \|\| head-&gt;next == NULL)<br>            return head;<br>        <br>        ListNode* prev = NULL;<br>        while (head != NULL) {<br>            ListNode* tmp = head-&gt;next;<br>            head-&gt;next = prev;<br>            prev = head;<br>            head = tmp;<br>        }<br>        return prev;<br>    }<br>};</code></pre> |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Given a sequence `1`, `1`, `2`, `3`, `5`, `8`, `13`, `21`..., where the indices start at `1`. For any given index, find the value of the sequence. For example, when the input is `3`, the output is `2`, and when the input is `6`, the output is `8`.<br>  <br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/fibonacci-number/description/)<br><br>At first, I used an array for storing the calculated results of the intermediate steps and built the array going forward. |  | **Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>int fib(int n){<br>  	int arr[n + 1];<br>  	arr[1] = 1;<br>  	arr[2] = 1;<br>  <br>  	int i;<br>  	for (i = 3; i &lt;= n; i++) {<br>          arr[i] = arr[i-1] + arr[i-2];<br>  	}<br>  	return arr[n];<br> }</code></pre><br><br>**Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>int fib(int n){<br>  	int a, b, c = 1;<br>  	a = 1;<br>  	b = 1;<br>  <br>  	int i;<br>  	for (i = 3; i &lt;= n; i++) {<br>          c =  a + b; <br>          a = b; <br>          b = c; <br>  	}<br>	return c;<br> }</code></pre><br><br>**Show Answer**:<br><pre><code> int fib(int n){<br>	if (n == 1)<br>            return 1;<br>	else if (n == 2)<br>            return 1;<br>	else<br>	    return fib(n-1) + fib(n-2);<br> }</code></pre> |

# SRBD

## Introduction
[Samsung R&D Institute Bangladesh (SRBD)](https://research.samsung.com/srbd) started its journey in February 2011. Located in the heart of Dhaka, it is the first ever R&D hub set up by a multinational company in Bangladesh.

> [!TIP]
> SRBD organizes a coding contest every year. The prize money is very handsome. Apart from that, if you do advance to round 2 or 3 means you might get a call for interview skipping the initial screening

> [!NOTE]
> SRBD employees have the opportunity to file patents for innovative work developed during their tenure.
## Interview Stages
SRBD takes interview in two round.

1. **Coding round:** Round 1 is coding round. The problems are typically from leetcode. You can only proceed to round 2 if you can solve round 1 correctly.

1. **Technical round:** Round 2 is a technical round. You might get asked about basic theories or solve some simple problems

## Coding Round Questions

You are asked to burst all the balloons.<br><br>If you burst the `ith` balloon, you will get `nums[i - 1] * nums[i] * nums[i + 1]` coins. If `i - 1` or `i + 1` goes out of bounds of the array, then treat it as if there is a balloon with a `1` painted on it.<br><br>Return the maximum coins you can collect by bursting the balloons wisely.<br><br>Variants: coins achieved is nums[i-1]*nums[i+1]<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/burst-balloons/description/) |  |  |

| A linked list of length `n` is given such that each node contains an additional random pointer, which could point to any node in the list, or null. Construct a deep copy of the list.<br><br>The deep copy should consist of exactly `n` brand new nodes, where each new node has its value set to the value of its corresponding original node. Both the next and random pointer of the new nodes should point to new nodes in the copied list such that the pointers in the original list and copied list represent the same list state. None of the pointers in the new list should point to nodes in the original list. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>class Solution {<br>public:<br>    unordered_map<Node*,Node*> random;<br>    Node* copyRandomList(Node* head) {<br>        if( head == NULL ) return NULL;<br>        Node* node = new Node(head->val);<br>        random[head] = node;<br>        node->next = copyRandomList(head->next);<br>        node->random = random[head->random];<br>        return node;<br>    }<br>};<br>``` |

| Mr. Kim has to deliver refrigerators to `N` customers. From the office, he is going to visit all the customers and then return to his home. <br>Each location of the office, his home, and the customers is given in the form of integer coordinates (x,y) (0≤x≤100, 0≤y≤100) . <br>The distance between two arbitrary locations (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is computed by \|x1-x2\| + \|y1-y2\|, where \|x\| denotes the absolute value <br>of x; for instance, \|3\|=\|-3\|=3. The locations of the office, his home, and the customers are all distinct. You should plan an optimal way <br>to visit all the N customers and return to his among all the possibilities. <br><br>You are given the locations of the office, Mr. Kim’s home, and the customers; the number of the customers is in the range of 5 to 10. <br><br>Write a program that, starting at the office, finds a (the) shortest path visiting all the customers and returning to his home. <br>Your program only have to report the distance of a (the) shortest path. |  |  |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |

| You are given a directed graph represented by an adjacency list. Your task is to detect if there exists a cycle in the graph.<br>If a cycle is found, print the nodes of the cycle in sorted order. Additionally, provide the results for each test case in the format <br>"#testCaseNo node1 node2 ... nodeK". If no cycle is present, print 0. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>#include<bits/stdc++.h><br>using namespace std;<br>int firstNodeOfTheCycle, lastNodeOfTheCycle;<br>bool detectCycle(int node, vector<vector<int>>& adjList, vector<bool> &visited, vector<bool> &dfsVisited, vector<int> &parent){<br>    visited[node] = true;<br>    dfsVisited[node] = true;<br><br>    for(auto neighbour : adjList[node]){<br>        if(!visited[neighbour]){<br>            parent[neighbour] = node;<br>            bool isCycleDetected = detectCycle(node, adjList, visited, dfsVisited, parent);<br>            if(isCycleDetected){<br>                firstNodeOfTheCycle = neighbour, lastNodeOfTheCycle = node;<br>                return true;<br>            }<br>        }<br>    }<br><br>    dfsVisited[node] = false;<br>    return false;<br>}<br><br>void calculateCycle(vector<int>& ans, vector<int>& parent){<br>    int curNode = lastNodeOfTheCycle;<br>    while(curNode != firstNodeOfTheCycle){<br>        ans.push_back(curNode);<br>        curNode = parent[curNode];<br>    }<br>    ans.push_back(curNode);<br>}<br>int main(){<br>    int tc = 10;<br>    for(int t = 1; t < tc; t++){<br>        int n,m;<br>        bool wasVisited = false;<br>        cin >> n >> m;<br>        vector<vector<int>>& adjList(n + 1);<br>        vector<bool> visited(n + 1, false), dfsVisited(n + 1, false);<br>        vector<int> parent(n + 1, -1), ans;<br>        for(int i = 0; i < m; i++){<br>            int u, v;<br>            cin >> u >> v;<br>            adjList[u].push_back(v);<br>        }<br>        for(int i = 1; i <= n; i++){<br>            if(!visited[i]){<br>                bool isCycleDetected = detectCycle(i, adjList, visited, dfsVisited, parent);<br>                if(isCycleDetected){<br>                    wasVisited = true;<br>                    calculateCycle(ans, parent);<br>                    sort(ans.begin(), ans.end());<br>                    cout<<"#"<<t;<br>                    for(auto it: ans){<br>                        cout<<" "<<it;<br>                    }<br>                    cout<<endl;<br>                    break;<br>                }<br>            }<br>        }<br>        if(!wasVisited){<br>            cout<<"#"<<t<<" "<<endl;<br>        }<br>    }<br>}<br>``` |

| You have string with repeated character. Example : `abcaade`. Count the number of characters that occur more than once. You can’t use array or map. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>[Answer 1]<br>My approach : Sort & search concurrent character `nlog(n)`.  <br><br>[Answer 2] <br>Optimize approach : An integer have 32 bit. We have 26 small letter character.  We just need to allocate each bit for position of a character. If an element is present set the concurrent bit and  finally count the number of set bit.  <br>(Comment from Tamim: Before trying this approach communicate with the interviewer to make sure about the characters present in the string ie only a-z is present and nothing else) |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Given 5 element. We want to search million time that will return if the searched element is present or no. What is the best time complexity. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>You might be tempted to answer using binary search on sorted list but it might be better to use linear search. The internal mechanism for binary search has some constant overhead. For small number that overhead overshadows the benefit of logarithmic complexity. I ran both linear search and binary search. Linear search is about 5 times faster than binary search |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Follow up question. We are guaranteed that each time new search element would be front the next position of previous search. Write code on pen & paper. |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>int lastElementPosition = 0;<br>int solve( vector<int> arr, int element){<br>	int l =  0 , r = lastElementPosition;<br>	while( l <= r){<br>		int mid = l + (r - l) / 2;<br>        if( arr[mid] == element ){<br>            lastElementPosition = mid;<br>            return mid;<br>        }<br>        else if( element < arr[mid] ){<br>            r = mid - 1;<br>        }else{<br>            lastElementPosition = l;<br>            l = mid + 1;<br>        }<br>    }<br>}<br>``` |

## On Campus Written Test

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |

| Given a list of courses you have to take and a list of prerequisites, return the order in which you have to take the courses. If it is not possible to take all the courses return an empty list.<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/course-schedule-ii) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>class Solution {<br>public:<br>    vector<int> order;<br>    vector<int> graph[2005];<br>    int color[2005] = {0};<br><br>    bool dfs(int u){<br>        color[u] = 1;<br>        for(auto v : graph[u]){<br>            if( color[v] == 1 ) return false;<br>            if( color[v] == 2 ) continue;<br>            bool possible = dfs(v);<br>            if( !possible ) return false;<br>        }<br>        color[u] = 2;<br>        order.push_back(u);<br>        return true;<br>    }<br><br>    vector<int> findOrder(int numCourses, vector<vector<int>>& prerequisites) {<br>        for(auto prerequisite : prerequisites){<br>            graph[prerequisite[1]].push_back(prerequisite[0]);<br>        }<br>        for(int i = 0; i < numCourses; i++){<br>            if( color[i] == 0 ) {<br>                bool possible = dfs(i);<br>                if( !possible ) return vector<int>{};<br>            }<br>        }<br>        reverse(order.begin(), order.end());<br>        return order;<br>    }<br>};<br>``` |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Given an integer array of size n, find all elements that appear more than `⌊n/3⌋` times.<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/majority-element-ii) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>class Solution {<br>public:<br>    vector<int> majorityElement(vector<int>& nums) {<br>        sort(nums.begin(),nums.end());<br>        vector<int> majors;<br>        int count = 1;<br><br>        for(int i = 1; i < nums.size(); i++){<br>            if( nums[i] != nums[i-1] ){<br>                if( count > nums.size()/3 ) majors.push_back(nums[i-1]);<br>                count = 0;<br>            }<br>            count++;<br>        }<br><br>        if( count > nums.size()/3 ) majors.push_back(nums.back());<br>        return majors;<br>    }<br>};<br>``` |

| Given string num representing a non-negative integer `num`, and an integer `k`, return the smallest possible integer after removing `k` digits from num.<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/remove-k-digits) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>class Solution {<br>public:<br>    string removeKdigits(string num, int k) {<br>       <br>        string newNum = "";<br>        // remove digits that are greater than the next digit<br>        for(int i=0;i<num.size();i++){<br>            while( k and newNum.size() and newNum.back() > num[i] ){<br>                k--;<br>                newNum.pop_back();<br>            }<br>            newNum += num[i];<br>        }<br>        // remove leading zeros<br>        reverse(newNum.begin(),newNum.end());<br>        while( newNum.size() and newNum.back() == '0' ) newNum.pop_back();<br>        reverse(newNum.begin(),newNum.end());<br>        // remove remaining k digits if any<br>        while(k and newNum.size()) {<br>            newNum.pop_back();<br>            k--;<br>        }<br>        // if newNum is empty, return 0<br>        if( newNum.size() == 0 ) newNum = "0";<br>        return newNum;<br>    }<br>};<br><br>``` |

# Therap

| <img width="441" height="1"> | <img width="441" height="1">       |
| :--------------------------- | :--------------------------------- |
| Founding year                | 2004                               |
| Company Website              | https://therapbd.com/              |
| Career Website               | https://therap.hire.trakstar.com/? |
| Technologies Used            | Java, J2EE                         |

## Introduction

[Therap (BD) Ltd.](https://therapbd.com/) is a US-based Software Company registered in Bangladesh, operating since 2004. They have their office in Dhaka, Bangladesh, and affiliate offices in the USA and Canada.
Therap has multiple roles.

1. Software Engineer
2. Database Engineer
3. Quality Assurance Engineer
4. Embedded System Engineer
5. System Adminstrator

> [!TIP]
> Therap BD organizes a Java fest every year. The prize money is very handsome. Apart from that, if you advance to final that means you get a direct ticket to interview skipping the initial screening

## Interview Stages

The selection process depends on the role, but generally has 3 to 4 stages,

1. **Initial screening:** This round is taken in written format
1. **1st technical round** The first round is taken by the BD team
1. **2nd technical round:** This round is typically taken by the USA team [not for all roles]
1. **HR Round:** This is the final stage before onboarding and typically deals with salary negotiation. 

## Interview Questions

- [Software Engineer Interview Questions](./swe)
- [Database Engineer Interview Questions](./dbe)
- [Machine Learning Engineer Interview Questions](./ml)
- [Associate System Adminstrator Interview Questions](./sys.md)

## Benefits, Perks and Things to Consider
- **Onboarding and Mentorship:** A three-six month paid probation period, in-house training program is provided for new hires.
- **Health Benefits:** Therap offers comprehensive health benefits and insurances to all employees.
- **Commuter Benefits:** Transportation facilities are provided for employees.
- **Flexible Work Arrangements:** Standard work hours are 9 AM – 5 PM, with Mondays designated as work-from-home days.
- **Meals and Snacks:** Free morning snacks, lunch, and evening snacks are provided daily.
- **Bonuses:** Two festival bonuses are offered each year.
- **Paid Time Off:** Employees receive 14 days of paid leave annually.
- **Wellness Programs:** An in-house gym and yoga sessions are available for employees (depends on team).
- **Company Retreats and Team Offsites:** Therap organizes an annual picnic and regular team outings.
- **Recreation Facilities:** A recreation room is equipped with games such as carrom, table tennis, and billiards.

| Given two binary tree. Check if they are identical [not isomorphism]<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/same-tree/) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>::: code-group<br><br>```C++ [Normal Solution]<br>bool isSameTree(TreeNode* p, TreeNode* q) {<br>    if( p == nullptr and q != nullptr ) return false;<br>    if( p != nullptr and q == nullptr ) return false;<br>    if( p == nullptr and q == nullptr ) return true;<br><br>    if( p->val != q->val ) return false;<br><br>    return isSameTree(p->left,q->left) &&<br>            isSameTree(p->right,q->right);<br>}<br><pre><code></code></pre>go [Weird Solution]<br>// ref: https://go.dev/tour/concurrency/7<br>package main<br><br>import (<br>	"fmt"<br><br>	"golang.org/x/tour/tree"<br>)<br><br>// Walk walks the tree t sending all values<br>// from the tree to the channel ch.<br>func WalkRecursive(t *tree.Tree, ch chan int) {<br>	if t.Left != nil {<br>		WalkRecursive(t.Left, ch)<br>	}<br>	ch <- t.Value<br>	if t.Right != nil {<br>		WalkRecursive(t.Right, ch)<br>	}<br>}<br><br>func Walk(t *tree.Tree, ch chan int) {<br>    WalkRecursive(t, ch)<br>    close(ch)<br>}<br><br>// Same determines whether the trees<br>// t1 and t2 contain the same values.<br>func Same(t1, t2 *tree.Tree) bool {<br>	ch1 := make(chan int)<br>	ch2 := make(chan int)<br>	go Walk(t1, ch1)<br>	go Walk(t2, ch2)<br>	for {<br>		x, ok1 := <-ch1<br>		y, ok2 := <-ch2<br><br>		if ok1 != ok2 \|\| x != y {<br>			return false<br>		}<br>		if !ok1 {<br>			break<br>		}<br>	}<br>	return true<br>}<br><br>func main() {<br>	fmt.Println(Same(tree.New(1), tree.New(2)))<br>}<br>```<br><br>::: |

| Find pairs with given target sum in a doubly linked list. <br><pre><code>Input: <br>1 &lt;&gt; 2 &lt;&gt; 4 &lt;&gt; 5 &lt;&gt; 6 &lt;&gt; 8 &lt;&gt; 9<br>target = 7<br>Output: <br>(1,6), (2,5)</code></pre><br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://www.geeksforgeeks.org/problems/find-pairs-with-given-sum-in-doubly-linked-list/1) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>```C++<br>class Solution<br>{<br>public:<br>    vector<pair<int, int>> findPairsWithGivenSum(Node *head, int target)<br>    {<br>        vector<pair<int,int>> ans;<br>        <br>        Node* left = head;<br>        <br>        /// traverse to the end of the list<br>        while(head!= nullptr && head->next!=nullptr){<br>            head = head->next;<br>        }<br>        Node* right = head;<br>        <br>        while(left!= right && left->prev != right){<br>            if(left->data + right->data == target){<br>                ans.push_back(make_pair(left->data, right->data));<br>                left = left->next;<br>                right = right->prev;<br>            }<br>            else if(left->data + right->data > target){<br>                right = right->prev;<br>            }else{<br>                left = left->next;<br>            }<br>        }<br>        <br>        return ans;    <br>        <br>    }<br>};<br>``` |

| Solve the problem using Object Oriented Programming<br><br>``` C++<br>int main(){<br>    int square1width = 50;<br>    int square2width = 80;<br>    int rectangle1width = 30, rectangle1height = 40;<br>    int rectangle2width = 20, rectangle2height = 40;<br><br>    int square1area = square1width* square1width;<br>    int square2area = square2width* square2width;<br>    int rectangle1area = rectangle1height*rectangle1width;<br>    int rectangle2area = rectangle2width* rectangle2height;<br>}<br>``` |  | **Show Answer**:<br>``` C++<br>#include <iostream><br>using namespace std;<br><br>// Abstract base class<br>class Shape {<br>public:<br>    virtual int area() const = 0;  // Pure virtual function for area<br>};<br><br>class Square : public Shape {<br>private:<br>    int width;<br>public:<br>    Square(int w) : width(w) {}  // Constructor to initialize width<br><br>    int area() const override {<br>        return width * width;  // Area of square<br>    }<br>};<br><br>class Rectangle : public Shape {<br>private:<br>    int width;<br>    int height;<br>public:<br>    Rectangle(int w, int h) : width(w), height(h) {}  // Constructor to initialize width and height<br><br>    int area() const override {<br>        return width * height;  // Area of rectangle<br>    }<br>};<br><br>int main() {<br>    <br>    Square square1(50);<br>    Square square2(80);<br>    Rectangle rectangle1(30, 40);<br>    Rectangle rectangle2(20, 40);<br><br>    cout << "Square 1 area: " << square1.area() << endl;<br>    cout << "Square 2 area: " << square2.area() << endl;<br>    cout << "Rectangle 1 area: " << rectangle1.area() << endl;<br>    cout << "Rectangle 2 area: " << rectangle2.area() << endl;<br>    return 0;<br>}<br><br>``` |

| A dictionary of sorted words was like this: [a, above, bad, broke, cat,..., yes, yolk, zoo]. After a malfunction it became this: [..., yes, yolk, zoo, a, above, bad, broke, cat,....]. Write a program so that given a word, one can find the word in the dictionary, with the same time complexity as when the dictionary was sorted.<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/search-in-rotated-sorted-array/description/) |  | **Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>def search(words, target):<br>        l, r = 0, len(words) - 1<br><br>        while l &lt;= r:<br>            m = (l + r) // 2<br>            if words[m] == target:<br>                return m<br>            <br>            if words[l] &lt;= words[m]:<br>                if target &lt; words[l] or target &gt; words[m]:<br>                    l = m + 1<br>                else :<br>                    r = m - 1<br>            <br>            else:<br>                if target &gt; words[r] or target &lt; words[m] :<br>                    r = m - 1<br>                else :<br>                    l = m + 1<br><br>        return -1</code></pre> |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| You are given a list of orders, where each order takes a certain amount of time to process (e.g., brewing a coffee). There are `k` identical machines available to process these orders. Orders are processed on a first-come, first-served basis, and each order must be assigned to exactly one machine. Orders will be placed sequentially. Find the total time required to complete all orders.<br><br><pre><code>Input: k = 2, orders = [3,2,5,4]<br>Output: 7</code></pre> |  | **Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>#include &lt;bits/stdc++.h&gt;<br>using namespace std;<br><br>class Solution {<br>public:<br>  int getMinTime(int k, vector&lt;int&gt; &amp;orders) {<br>    // Min-heap to keep track of the earliest available machine<br>    priority_queue&lt;int, vector&lt;int&gt;, greater&lt;int&gt;&gt; pq;<br><br>    for (int curr : orders) {<br>      if (!pq.empty() \|\| pq.size() &lt; k) {<br>        // If there are still empty machines, just push the task time<br>        pq.push(curr);<br>      } else {<br>        // If pq.size() == k, get the earliest available machine<br>        int x = pq.top();<br>        pq.pop();<br>        // Add the current task time to that machine and push back<br>        pq.push(x + curr);<br>      }<br>    }<br><br>    // At the end, return the max element in the pq while pq is not empty<br>    int max_time = 0;<br>    while (!pq.empty()) {<br>      max_time =<br>          pq.top(); // Since it&#039;s a min-heap, the last element popped is the max<br>      pq.pop();<br>    }<br><br>    return max_time;<br>  }<br>};</code></pre> |

| Given an encoded string, return its decoded string.<br><br>The encoding rule is: `k[encoded_string]`, where the `encoded_string` inside the square brackets is being repeated exactly `k` times. Note that `k` is guaranteed to be a positive integer. There can be nested encoding. You may assume that the input string is always valid; digits are only used as repeat numbers.<br><br><pre><code>Input: s = &quot;3[a2[bc]]&quot;<br>Output: &quot;abcbcabcbcabcbc&quot;</code></pre><br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/decode-string/description/?envType=problem-list-v2&envId=recursion) |  | **Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>class Solution {<br>public:<br>  string decodeString(string s) {<br>    stack&lt;char&gt; st;<br><br>    for (int i = 0; i &lt; s.size(); i++) {<br>      if (s[i] == &#039;]&#039;) {<br>        string substr = &quot;&quot;;<br>        while (st.top() != &#039;[&#039;) {<br>          substr += st.top();<br>          st.pop();<br>        }<br>        st.pop();<br><br>        reverse(substr.begin(), substr.end());<br>        string num = &quot;&quot;;<br><br>        while (!st.empty() &amp;&amp; isdigit(st.top())) {<br>          num += st.top();<br>          st.pop();<br>        }<br>        reverse(num.begin(), num.end());<br>        int n = stoi(num);<br><br>        string temp = substr;<br>        for (int j = 1; j &lt; n; j++) {<br>          substr += temp;<br>        }<br><br>        for (int j = 0; j &lt; substr.size(); j++) {<br>          st.push(substr[j]);<br>        }<br><br>      } else {<br>        st.push(s[i]);<br>      }<br>    }<br><br>    string ans = &quot;&quot;;<br>    while (!st.empty()) {<br>      ans += st.top();<br>      st.pop();<br>    }<br><br>    reverse(ans.begin(), ans.end());<br>    return ans;<br>  }<br>};</code></pre> |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Given two strings `s` and `t`, return the shortest substring of `s` such that every character in `t`, including duplicates, is present in the substring. If such a substring does not exist, return an empty string `""`.<br><br><pre><code>Input: s = &quot;OUZODYXAZV&quot;, t = &quot;XYZ&quot;<br>Output: &quot;YXAZ&quot;</code></pre><br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://neetcode.io/problems/minimum-window-with-characters/question?list=neetcode150) |  | **Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>class Solution {<br>public:<br>  string minWindow(string s, string t) {<br>    map&lt;char, int&gt; pattern, mp;<br><br>    for (auto &amp;x : t)<br>      pattern[x]++;<br><br>    int i = 0, j = 0, cnt = 0, n = t.size();<br>    string ans = &quot;&quot;;<br><br>    while (i &lt; s.size()) {<br>      mp[s[i]]++;<br><br>      if (mp[s[i]] &lt;= pattern[s[i]])<br>        cnt++;<br><br>      while (j &lt;= i &amp;&amp; mp[s[j]] &gt; pattern[s[j]]) {<br>        mp[s[j]]--;<br>        j++;<br>      }<br><br>      if (cnt == n &amp;&amp; (ans.empty() \|\| i - j + 1 &lt; ans.length()))<br>        ans = s.substr(j, i - j + 1);<br><br>      i++;<br>    }<br><br>    return ans;<br>  }<br>};</code></pre> |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Given an integer array `nums`, return the number of triplets chosen from the array that can make triangles if we take them as side lengths of a triangle.<br><br><pre><code>Input: nums = [2,2,3,4]<br>Output: 3</code></pre><br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/valid-triangle-number/description/) |  | **Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>class Solution {<br>public:<br>    int triangleNumber(vector&lt;int&gt;&amp; nums) {<br>        sort(nums.begin(), nums.end());<br>        int count = 0;<br>        for (int i = nums.size() - 1; i &gt;= 2; i--) {<br>            int l = 0, r = i - 1;<br>            while (l &lt; r) {<br>                if (nums[l] + nums[r] &gt; nums[i]) {<br>                    count += r - l;<br>                    r--;<br>                } else {<br>                    l++;<br>                }<br>            }<br>        }<br>        return count;<br>    }<br>};</code></pre> |

| You are given a singly linked list. Traverse the list and reverse every block of 3 consecutive nodes. If the number of nodes is not a multiple of 3, the remaining nodes at the end should be left as is.<br><br><pre><code>Input:  a &gt; b &gt; c &gt; d &gt; e &gt; f &gt; g &gt; h<br>Output: c &gt; b &gt; a &gt; f &gt; e &gt; d &gt; g &gt; h</code></pre><br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/reverse-nodes-in-k-group/) |  | **Show Answer**:<br><pre><code>ListNode* reverseKGroup(ListNode* head, int k) {<br>    ListNode* curr = head;<br>    int count = 0;<br>    while (curr &amp;&amp; count &lt; k) {<br>        curr = curr-&gt;next;<br>        count++;<br>    }<br>    if (count &lt; k) return head;<br><br>    curr = head;<br>    ListNode* prev = nullptr;<br>    for (int i = 0; i &lt; k; i++) {<br>        ListNode* next = curr-&gt;next;<br>        curr-&gt;next = prev;<br>        prev = curr;<br>        curr = next;<br>    }<br>    head-&gt;next = reverseKGroup(curr, k);<br>    return prev;<br>}</code></pre> |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Briefly explain ACID properties |  | **Show Answer**:<br>ACID is a set of properties of database transactions intended to guarantee data validity despite errors, power failures, and other mishaps. Databases that support this are called ACID compliance. The properties are<br><br>- **Atomicity:** Each statement in a transaction (to read, write, update or delete data) is treated as a single unit. Either the entire statement is executed, or none of it is executed.<br>- **Consistency:** Ensures the databases remain consistent following some predefined business logic both before and after the transaction<br>- **Isolation:** Each transaction executes in such a way that one is not affected by other s though they were occurring only one.<br>- **Durability:** The data changes by a successfull transaction is saved even in the event of system failure<br><br>> [!IMPORTANT]<br>> Atomicity, isolation and durability are properties of the database, whereas consistency is a property of the application. The C in ACID was tossed in to make the acronym work. [ref: Martin Kleppmann, Designing Data Intensive Applications] |

## Introduction
[WeDevs](https://wedevs.com/) is an international software company. weDevs is the parent company of Dokan Multivendor, WP User Frontend, WP Project Manager, WP ERP, weMail, FlyWP, and six more exclusive WordPress products. At weDevs, they are dedicated to pushing the boundaries of what is possible with WordPress and SaaS solutions.

## Interview Stages
weDevs' fresher recruitment is currently based on competitive programming. They post job circulars on their career page and LinkedIn page. The circulars often ask for a good national rank or a Codeforces rating of 1600+ (you can apply if you are slightly below this).

Their interview process consists of three stages. The first two stages vary from person to person. For some, the first stage is a basic verbal session focusing on fundamental computer science concepts, while for others, it is a coding round. The final stage is called the HR round. To receive an offer, you must pass all three stages.

# WellDev Ltd

| <img width="441" height="1"> | <img width="441" height="1"> |
| :-| :- |
| Founding year | |
| Company Website | https://www.welldev.io/ |
| Career Website | https://www.welldev.io/careers |

## Introduction

WellDev Ltd is a software company based in Zurich, Switzerland, specializing in software development and IT services. It has offices in Bangladesh (Dhaka), South Africa, Canada etc.

## Interview Stages

1. **Initial Screening:** After dropping CV they take a MCQ Round, almost every candidate gets an email for participation in this round.
2. **Round 1:** The first round is a technical round and generally lasts for an hour.
3. **Round 2:** The second round is divided into two part. The first part is a behavioural part taken by HR of the company. For the second part two software engineer conducts the technical sessions.
4. **COO Round:** The final round is taken by the COO of the company

## MCQ Round

A Broad Range of Topics

This round consisted of a multiple-choice questionnaire covering these topics. The test required me to share my screen with Quillgo and keep my camera on. It covers wide range of topics like JavaScript fundamentals, OOP, DBMS, SWE principles, Networking, Rest API knowledge, Analytical reasoning, DSA (time complexity, sorting, binary trees, MST, greedy algorithms).

## First Round Questions

Hands-On Problem Solving

| What will the output of this code in C Programming Language and why?<br><br><pre><code>int arr[3] = {1, 2, 3};<br><br>if(&amp;arr[0] == &amp;arr){<br>    printf(&quot;They are the same!&quot;);<br>}else {<br>    printf(&quot;Not same&quot;);<br>}</code></pre> |  | **Show Answer**:<br>The name of the array is a pointer to the first item of the Array.<br>So it will print "They are the same!" |

| Tab1 | Show Description | Short Answer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Given an array, what will be the base address if we print the array name only (e.g., printf(ara))? |  | **Show Answer**:<br>It will print the address of the first item of the array.<br>In C, you can't pass array to functions by "Pass by value".<br>So when you pass an array to an function (for example: printf(arr)),<br>the compiler will actually pass the pointer to the first element. <br>You can easily test this hypothesis by doing something like this:<br><br><pre><code>int arr[1] = {100};<br><br>if(arr == &amp;arr){<br>    printf(&quot;Yay!\n&quot;);<br>}<br><br>if(arr == &amp;arr[0]){<br>    printf(&quot;Damn!&quot;);<br>}</code></pre><br><br>The above code should print<br><br><pre><code>Yay!<br>Damn!</code></pre> |

| What are the ACID properties in DBMS? |  | **Show Answer**:<br>ACID is a set of properties of database transactions intended to guarantee data validity despite errors, power failures, and other mishaps. Databases that support this are called ACID compliance. The properties are<br><br>- **Atomicity:** Each statement in a transaction (to read, write, update or delete data) is treated as a single unit. Either the entire statement is executed, or none of it is executed.<br>- **Consistency:** Ensures the databases remain consistent following some predefined business logic both before and after the transaction<br>- **Isolation:** Each transaction executes in such a way that one is not affected by other s though they were occurring only one.<br>- **Durability:** The data changes by a successfull transaction is saved even in the event of system failure<br><br>> [!IMPORTANT]<br>> Atomicity, isolation and durability are properties of the database, whereas consistency is a property of the application. The C in ACID was tossed in to make the acronym work. [ref: Martin Kleppmann, Designing Data Intensive Applications] |

| Given an array of integers heights representing the histogram's bar height where the width of each bar is 1, return the area of the largest rectangle in the histogram.<br><br>[**💻 Submit Code**](https://leetcode.com/problems/largest-rectangle-in-histogram/description/) |  | **Show Answer**:<br>::: code-group<br><br>```C++ [Stack]<br>// src: https://www.geeksforgeeks.org/largest-rectangular-area-in-a-histogram-using-stack/<br>int largestRectangleArea(vector<int>& hist) {<br>    int n = hist.size();<br>    stack<int> s;<br><br>    int max_area = 0;<br>    int tp;<br>    int area_with_top;<br>    int i = 0;<br>    while (i < n) {<br>        if (s.empty() \|\| hist[s.top()] <= hist[i]){<br>            s.push(i++);<br>        } else {<br>            tp = s.top();<br>            s.pop();<br><br>            area_with_top = hist[tp] * (s.empty() ? i : i - s.top() - 1);<br>            max_area = max(max_area,area_with_top);<br>        }<br>    }<br><br>    while (s.empty() == false) {<br>        tp = s.top();<br>        s.pop();<br><br>        area_with_top = hist[tp] * (s.empty() ? i : i - s.top() - 1);<br>        max_area = max(max_area,area_with_top);<br>    }<br><br>    return max_area;<br>}<br><pre><code></code></pre>C++ [Segment Tree]<br>#include <bits/stdc++.h><br>using namespace std;<br>#define ll long long<br>#define pii pair<ll,ll><br>#define F first<br>#define S second<br>const int MAX = 1e9+5;<br>const int N = 200005;<br>pii segtree[4*N];<br>int ara[N],n;<br><br>void build(int node,int l,int r ){<br>    if( l == r ){<br>        segtree[node] = {ara[l],l};<br>        return;<br>    }<br>    int mid = (l+r)/2;<br>    build(node*2,l,mid);<br>    build(node*2+1,mid+1,r);<br>    segtree[node] = min( segtree[node*2],segtree[node*2+1] );<br>}<br><br>pii query(int node,int L,int R,int l,int r){<br>    if( l>R or r<L ) return {MAX,-1};<br>    if( l>=L and r<=R ) return segtree[node];<br>    int mid = (l+r)/2;<br>    return min( query(node*2,L,R,l,mid), query(node*2+1,L,R,mid+1,r) );<br>}<br><br>ll getRect(int l,int r){<br>    if( l>r ) return 0;<br>    auto pp = query(1,l,r,0,n-1);<br>    ll res = (r-l+1)*pp.F;<br>    return max({ res, getRect(l,pp.S-1),getRect(pp.S+1,r) });<br>}<br>int main(){<br>    cin>>n;<br>    for(int i=0;i<n;i++) cin>>ara[i];<br>    build(1,0,n-1);<br><br>    cout<<getRect(0,n-1);<br>}<br>```<br><br>::: |

# Competitions for CSE Students

Some of the competitions that a CSE Student of Bangladesh can participate during his undergrad life. Some of them allow students below undergrad or above it too.

The information are incomplete, can be wrong and in no way are official from the organizers. Some of the competitions might not occur in the future too. 

This is just a list created from what I recall about past competitions. 

If there are any mistakes that needs to be corrected create a pull request or raise a issue. I'll fix that.
